126	126.7	100.5	Ma	This study	Asia	China	Lower Cretaceous Jiabula Formation at the Zhangda section (28.9°N, 91.1°E), Nagarze, northern subzone of the Tethyan Himalaya, southern Tibet. The formation is about 200 m thick here; the paleomagnetic samples are from grey, medium-bedded limestone of its lower part: 242 cores at 27 sites (ZL01 to ZL27), whose beds dip 45 to 87° to the northwest. Table S3 of the supplement gives the high-temperature component of 204 specimens, without site means. The authors average the directions of the specimens, so each specimen is entered as its own site, with the same name as sample and as specimen. The published results are the rows with a result name: the high-temperature component tilt-corrected (with the pole) and in situ, and the low-temperature component in situ. Age ca. 126 Ma, the age the authors use. Older bound 126.7 Ma: the oldest of the youngest zircon dates (126.7 to 125.8 Ma, LA-ICP-MS U-Pb) of a sandstone and an andesite from the upper part of the section, which the authors interpret as the maximum depositional age of the formation; the sampled limestones are in the lower part of the section. Younger bound 100.5 Ma: the top of the Lower Cretaceous (International Chronostratigraphic Chart v2024/12); the paper assigns the formation to the Lower Cretaceous, below the Upper Cretaceous Zongzhuo Formation, and gives no younger numerical bound. 126	126.7	100.5	Ma	This study	Asia	China	Published mean of the high-temperature component after tilt correction, and the pole of the study. The mean is the Fisher mean of the 204 specimen directions of Table S3, which gives 140.5/60.0, k 25.7, α95 2.0. The published pole (11.2°N, 300.5°E, A95 2.6°) is the pole of that mean direction at the section: the published mean gives 11.2°N, 300.5°E with dp 2.3° and dm 3.0°, and √(dp·dm) = 2.6° is the printed A95. The Fisher mean of the 204 specimen VGPs is 9.3°N, 299.8°E (A95 2.6°), 2.0° from the published pole. All directions are of reversed polarity; the authors conclude that the magnetization was acquired in the reverse polarity chron of M3. Fold tests positive: McElhinny (1964), ks/kg = 1.81 against 1.26 at the 1 percent point; McFadden (1990), ξ2 94.62 in situ and 5.44 tilt-corrected against a critical value of 23.49 at 99 percent; Watson and Enkin (1993), maximum k 25.6 at 100 percent unfolding. Inclination shallowing: an elongation/inclination analysis gives a corrected inclination of 63.0° (59.0 to 69.0°) for all directions, and 61.0° (59.0 to 64.0°) for the 197 left by a 45° cutoff; the authors conclude that no significant shallowing occurred and use the uncorrected mean. Recalculated with the function ipmag.find_ei of PmagPy (1000 bootstrap samples), the corrected inclination of the 204 directions is about 63° (60 to 69°), the median and 95 percent bounds of the bootstrap samples that have a solution, in agreement with the published values; the numbers vary with the random bootstrap. tab 	ages
age	age_high	age_low	age_unit	citations	description	location	method_codes
126	126.7	100.5	Ma	This study	Age ca. 126 Ma, the age the authors use. Older bound 126.7 Ma: the oldest of the youngest zircon dates (126.7 to 125.8 Ma, LA-ICP-MS U-Pb) of a sandstone and an andesite from the upper part of the section, which the authors interpret as the maximum depositional age of the formation; the sampled limestones are in the lower part of the section. Younger bound 100.5 Ma: the top of the Lower Cretaceous (International Chronostratigraphic Chart v2024/12); the paper assigns the formation to the Lower Cretaceous, below the Upper Cretaceous Zongzhuo Formation, and gives no younger numerical bound. The sandstone (WMD01) gave 82 detrital zircon dates, the two youngest 126.2 and 126.7 Ma; the andesite (SX162-165) gave 10 concordant dates, the youngest 125.8 Ma.	Jiabula Formation, Zhangda section	GM-UPB:GM-CC-STRAT
